| 542 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! Look at this tool shelf. There are so many tools! What's this tool? These are scissors. What are they used for? It's used for cutting paper. Say it with me. Reread. Rewrite. Rebuild. Tell, retell. Read, reread. Write, rewrite. Build, rebuild. Re means again. What's this? It's tape. It's used for sticking things. Now, look around your room. Find a tool. It's a hammer. It's used for hitting nails. What's this? It's a screwdriver. It's used for turning screws. Remember, tools can be dangerous. At home, always ask an adult before you use them. Now, phonics. Retell. Reread. Rewrite. Rebuild. Tell, retell. Read, reread. Write, rewrite. Build, rebuild. Re means again. What's this? It's tape. It's used for sticking things together. Picture. Culture. Mixture. Gesture. The t u r e at the end says cher. Picture, not pic-ture-e. Let's make a gesture. Wave your hand! Now, tell me. Close your eyes. What can you hear? I can hear the drums. Let's go and see the festival! | Hello, everyone! Welcome to World 20, Festival Island! This is the last world of Stage 2. Listen. Boom, boom, boom! What's that sound? | 572 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! Yesterday, we arrived on Festival Island. Today, a boy on the beach gave Birimo a flyer. The festival is on Saturday! I want to ask you nicely. Would you like to come to the festival? Say it with me. Would you like to come to the festival? Nice! Would you like to is a polite way to invite someone. It's softer than Do you want to. | 575 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Let's make invitation cards for our festival friends. An invitation tells three things. When? Where? What? Listen. Treasure, zher. Cure, yoor. Future, cher. Draw a picture of a lantern on your card! One more thing. Never write your home address on the internet. Paper cards for friends are fine. | 578 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! In autumn, many countries have a harvest festival. Harvest means picking rice, fruit, and vegetables from the fields. At the festival, we say thank you for the food. Say it with me. We say thank you for the food. Wonderful! Farmers grow rice and vegetables. In Japan, too, there are autumn festivals for the rice harvest. しゅうかくさいは たべものに ありがとうを いう おまつり。 Let's practice. We say thank you to the farmers. We say thank you for the rain and the sun. Now, phonics. Dear. Deer. They sound the same, but the spelling is different. Dear has e a r. Deer has e e r. A deer is an animal. Dear is for letters, like Dear Grandma. Appear. Engineer. Now, tell me. What food do you want to say thank you for? | Hello, everyone! In the fall, many countries have a harvest festival. Harvest means picking rice, fruit, and vegetables from the fields. | 581 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today, we're visiting my aunt's house on the island. When guests come, hosts say, Come on in. Make yourself at home. Say it with me. Make yourself at home. Lovely! It means, relax and feel comfortable here, like in your own home. Make yourself at home. は「くつろいでね」と いう もてなしの ことば。 Guests can say, Thank you. What a lovely house! Let's practice. Come on in. Please take off your shoes here. Please sit down. Would you like a cushion? Guests say, Thank you. Excuse me. Hosts say, Please make yourself at home. Relax! Now, phonics. About. Again. Away. Alone. The first a is very weak. It sounds like uh. Uh-bout. Uh-gain. This weak sound is very common in English. Now, I'm your guest. Knock, knock! What do you say? | Hello, everyone! Today, we're visiting my aunt's house on the island. When guests come, hosts say, Come on in. Make yourself at home. Say it with me. Make yourself at home. Great! It means, relax and feel comfortable here, like in your own home. Make yourself at home. は「くつろいでね」と いう もてなしの ことば。 Guests can say, Thank you. What a lovely house! Let's practice. Come on in. Please take off your shoes here. Please sit down. Would you like a cushion? Guests say, Thank you so much. Hosts say, Please make yourself at home. Relax! Now, phonics. About. Again. Away. Alone. The first a is very weak. It sounds like uh. Uh-bout. Uh-gain. This weak sound is very common in English. Now, I'm your guest. Knock, knock! What do you say? | A+B | 'Lovely!' as praise is British (block-wide rule) and blurs the vocab word 'lovely'; 'Excuse me' when entering a home is a calque of おじゃまします |

| 583 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! It's time to leave my aunt's house. When we leave a friend's house, we say thank you. Thank you for having me. Say it with me. Thank you for having me. Perfect! Having me means inviting me to your house. Then, add, I had a great time. The host says, Please come again! よその いえを でる ときは Thank you for having me. と いおう。 Let's practice. Thank you for the delicious dinner. Thank you for showing me your garden. I had a great time. Thank you for the lovely lemon tea, too. Now, phonics. Lemon. Melon. Seven. Garden. The o and the e are weak here. Lem-un. Sev-un. A weak sound can be spelled with any vowel letter. Now, say goodbye to me. What do you say when you leave? | Hello, everyone! It's time to leave my aunt's house. When we leave a friend's house, we say thank you. Thank you for having me. Say it with me. Thank you for having me. Perfect! Having me means inviting me to your house. Then, add, I had a great time. The host says, Please come again! よその いえを でる ときは Thank you for having me. と いおう。 Let's practice. Thank you for the delicious cake. Thank you for showing me your garden. I had a great time. Thank you for the lovely lemon tea, too. Now, phonics. Lemon. Melon. Seven. Garden. The o and the e are weak here. Lem-un. Sev-un. A weak sound can be spelled with any vowel letter. Now, say goodbye to me. What do you say when you leave? | A+B | The guests had tea and banana cake, not dinner (L582 story) |

| 598 | `teacherScript` | Good evening, everyone! Tonight is rehearsal night. Here are three tips for a great show. Speak slowly. Look at the audience. Say it with me. Speak slowly. Look at the audience. Perfect! Tip number three. Take a deep breath and smile. Are you nervous? It's okay to be nervous. Everyone feels nervous before a show, even teachers. きんちょうしても だいじょうぶ。ゆっくり はなそう。 Let's practice. Speak loudly. Don't speak too fast. Look at your friends' faces. Speak slowly, and smile. If you forget a word, that's okay. Take a breath and try again. Your friends will help you. We are a team! Now, phonics review. Learn. Festival. Bridge. Waving. Now, stand up and say your first sentence. Speak slowly. Look at me. Great job! You're ready for tomorrow. I believe in you! | Good evening, everyone! Tonight is rehearsal night. Here are three tips for a great show. Speak slowly. Look at the audience. Say it with me. Speak slowly. Look at the audience. Perfect! Tip number three. Take a deep breath and smile. Are you nervous? Speak slowly, and smile. If you forget a word, that's OK. Take a breath and try again. Your friends will help you. We are a team! Now, phonics review. Learn. Festival. Bridge. Waving.
